So... that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice this problem with your speech becoming jumbled like this? Was it something that started recently, or has this been happening for a long time?\n\nPatient: I suppose... maybe six months. Just... out on the sales floor. That's when it started getting noticeable, I guess. The... the jumbled parts, and when I just stop. About half a year now.\n\nDoctor: Besides the jumbled speech, have you noticed any changes in your memory, your ability to think clearly, or how you handle everyday tasks over these past six months?\n\nPatient: Well... work has been hard. They... they don't let me run the weekend shifts anymore. Too busy, I guess. My boss... we had a review. He said I might get suspended if I don't... fix things. \n\nAnd at home... I don't know. My wife just does more now. The things I used to do. People just... they get uncomfortable around me. Or confused. So she just handles it instead. It's just... hard to manage things right now.\n\nDoctor: Over these past six months, have you noticed any problems with your memory—like forgetting things you usually remember, or having trouble keeping track of information?\n\nPatient: Memory? I... I don't think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that. Just... what I told you before. The talking part. But remembering things... I think that's fine.\n\nDoctor: Before these past six months, going back to when you were younger—did you ever have any trouble speaking clearly, or did teachers or family ever mention problems with how you talked or put words together?\n\nPatient: No. Nothing like that. Not... not when I was younger. Just... just recently. Like I said.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent illnesses, infections, high fevers, head injuries, or times when you felt confused or disoriented—even briefly—in the past six months?\n\nPatient: No. I haven't been sick. No head injuries or... or fevers. Just... just what I've told you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever lost consciousness, had a seizure, or had a period where you weren't aware of what was happening around you during these past six months?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't passed out or anything. No seizures. I... I know what's going on around me. Just... no.\n\nDoctor: Has your ability to speak been getting worse over these six months, or has it stayed about the same since it started?\n\nPatient: It's... it's just about the same, I guess. Hasn't really changed. Just... the same.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you walk, your balance, your coordination, or any tremors or stiffness in your body over these past six months?\n\nPatient: No... I walk fine. I haven't noticed any shaking or... anything like that. It's just normal.\n\nDoctor: When you're trying to explain something at work, do you have trouble finding the right words to use, or is it more that the words come out in the wrong order—like the sentences don't make sense?\n\nPatient: I guess... it's more that they just jump around. Like, if I'm trying to explain the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule... I'll start talking about the trucks, and then... Thursdays. And then maybe the church grill, or my boss's tone. I don't know. People just look at me like they can't follow what I'm saying. That's mostly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: When you were in school as a child, did your teachers ever say anything about your reading, writing, or how you expressed yourself in class—even if you don't remember having problems yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I don't... I don't remember anything like that. School was just school. No one said anything.\n\nDoctor: Besides the jumbled speech, have you noticed any difficulty with understanding what other people say to you, or any trouble with reading or writing over these past six months?\n\nPatient: No... I understand what people say just fine. And reading and writing... I haven't had any trouble with that. It's really just... what I told you before.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I... I don't think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that. Just... what I told you before. The talking part. But remembering things... I think that's fine.\n\nDoctor: Before these past six months, going back to when you were younger—did you ever have any trouble speaking clearly, or did teachers or family ever mention problems with how you talked or put words together?\n\nPatient: No. Nothing like that. Not... not when I was younger. Just... just recently. Like I said.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent illnesses, infections, high fevers, head injuries, or times when you felt confused or disoriented—even briefly—in the past six months?\n\nPatient: No. I haven't been sick. No head injuries or... or fevers. Just... just what I've told you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever lost consciousness, had a seizure, or had a period where you weren't aware of what was happening around you during these past six months?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't passed out or anything. No seizures. I... I know what's going on around me. Just... no.\n\nDoctor: Has your ability to speak been getting worse over these six months, or has it stayed about the same since it started?\n\nPatient: It's... it's just about the same, I guess. Hasn't really changed. Just... the same.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you walk, your balance, your coordination, or any tremors or stiffness in your body over these past six months?\n\nPatient: No... I walk fine. I haven't noticed any shaking or... anything like that. It's just normal.\n\nDoctor: When you're trying to explain something at work, do you have trouble finding the right words to use, or is it more that the words come out in the wrong order—like the sentences don't make sense?\n\nPatient: I guess... it's more that they just jump around. Like, if I'm trying to explain the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule... I'll start talking about the trucks, and then... Thursdays. And then maybe the church grill, or my boss's tone. I don't know. People just look at me like they can't follow what I'm saying. That's mostly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: When you were in school as a child, did your teachers ever say anything about your reading, writing, or how you expressed yourself in class—even if you don't remember having problems yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I don't... I don't remember anything like that. School was just school. No one said anything.\n\nDoctor: Besides the jumbled speech, have you noticed any difficulty with understanding what other people say to you, or any trouble with reading or writing over these past six months?\n\nPatient: No... I understand what people say just fine. And reading and writing... I haven't had any trouble with that. It's really just... what I told you before.\n\nDoctor: Over these past six months, have you noticed any changes in how you think or reason through problems, or any new difficulty with things like planning your day or making decisions?\n\nPatient: No... not really. My wife just does more now. The things I used to do. People just... they get uncomfortable around me. Or confused. So she just handles it instead. It's just... hard to manage things right now.\n\nDoctor: Over these past six months, have you noticed any problems with your memory—like forgetting things you usually remember, or having trouble keeping track of information?\n\nPatient: Memory? I... I don't think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that. Just... what I told you before. The talking part. But remembering things... I think that's fine.\n\nDoctor: Before these past six months, going back to when you were younger—did you ever have any trouble speaking clearly, or did teachers or family ever mention problems with how you talked or put words together?\n\nPatient: No. Nothing like that. Not... not when I was younger. Just... just recently. Like I said.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent illnesses, infections, high fevers, head injuries, or times when you felt confused or disoriented—even briefly—in the past six months?\n\nPatient: No. I haven't been sick. No head injuries or... or fevers. Just... just what I've told you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever lost consciousness, had a seizure, or had a period where you weren't aware of what was happening around you during these past six months?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't passed out or anything. No seizures. I... I know what's going on around me. Just... no.\n\nDoctor: Has your ability to speak been getting worse over these six months, or has it stayed about the same since it started?\n\nPatient: It's... it's just about the same, I guess. Hasn't really changed. Just... the same.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you walk, your balance, your coordination, or any tremors or stiffness in your body over these past six months?\n\nPatient: No... I walk fine. I haven't noticed any shaking or... anything like that. It's just normal.\n\nDoctor: When you're trying to explain something at work, do you have trouble finding the right words to use, or is it more that the words come out in the wrong order—like the sentences don't make sense?\n\nPatient: I guess... it's more that they just jump around. Like, if I'm trying to explain the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule... I'll start talking about the trucks, and then... Thursdays. And then maybe the church grill, or my boss's tone. I don't know. People just look at me like they can't follow what I'm saying. That's mostly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: When you were in school as a child, did your teachers ever say anything about your reading, writing, or how you expressed yourself in class—even if you don't remember having problems yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I don't... I don't remember anything like that. School was just school. No one said anything.\n\nDoctor: Besides the jumbled speech, have you noticed any difficulty with understanding what other people say to you, or any trouble with reading or writing over these past six months?\n\nPatient: No... I understand what people say just fine. And reading and writing... I haven't had any trouble with that. It's really just... what I told you before.\n\nDoctor: Over these past six months, have you noticed any changes in how you think or reason through problems, or any new difficulty with things like planning your day or making decisions?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I just do what I need to do at work. Decisions are fine. I haven't noticed any changes like that.\n\nDoctor: When you were growing up, did you ever feel like you had to work harder than other kids to keep up with conversations, or did anyone ever say you were a 'late talker' or had trouble with speech when you were very young—even if it seemed to improve?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. My parents never mentioned anything like that. I talked fine, as far as I know.\n\nDoctor: When you were a child or teenager, did you ever need extra help in school with any subject, or did you ever attend any special classes or tutoring—even if it was just for a short time?\n\nPatient: No. I didn't need any extra help. I did fine in school. Just regular classes.\n\nDoctor: I want to go back to something. When you were in your twenties or thirties, before these past six months, did anyone ever comment that you had an unusual way of speaking, or did you ever feel like organizing your thoughts into words was harder for you than it seemed to be for other people?\n\nPatient: No... nobody ever said anything like that.
